Document 304: France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 8 October 2026

EcoFlash

France: double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and 
stable non-performing loans ratio

Business creations rose by 12% y/y over the first eight months of 2026 1. This is significantly higher than the 5.3% y/y 
growth recorded in 2025. This increase was driven by sectors linked to information and communication technologies.

Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 5.3% y/y between January and July, according to data from the Banque de 
France (compared with the 3.6% y/y increase for the whole of 2025). The rise was more pronounced in the 1st quarter (+8% y/y) 
before slowing down. This slowdown could persist, as, according to Altares, payment delays have fallen to an average of 12.3 
days in the second quarter of 2026, compared with 14.1 days a year earlier (and 13.1 days for the European average).

Rising bankruptcies are more heavily affecting the services sector, which is also experiencing slightly stronger growth 
in business creations. This correlation is particularly noticeable in the retail sector, as business creations have risen by 13% 
since the start of 2026, while bankruptcies are up by 3%. In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been 
falling since the second quarter of 2026.

The average ratio of non -performing corporate loans has remained stable at a moderately high level for the past year  
(4.0% of total loans and advances in Q2 2026). In construction, where activity is more cyclical than in other sectors, the ra tio of 
non-performing loans has reached its highest level since 2019, standing at 9.5%.

The proportion of loans that have deteriorated but are not yet non-performing (“Stage 2” according to IFRS 9 terminology) 
is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all -time high (16.1%). This indicator, which helps to anticipate trends in 
non-performing loans, is relatively encouraging.

Despite a significant increase since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies remains, on average, below its peak in 
Q1 2025 (0.53 compared with 0.61, respectively). This indicator generally allows bankruptcies to be weighted based on the size 
of the businesses. However, the economic weight of bankrupted small businesses is at a historically high level (2.11).

Take a look at our EcoCharts on trends in business creations and bankruptcies in France and the 
associated risks. It will be updated on a quarterly basis.

Thomas Humblot 
Economiste, team: Banking economics 
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced economies, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

1 For more details, see Business creations and bankruptcies in France: double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, 
and stable non-performing loans, 8 October 2026.

Document 305: France’s unrest adds to an already toxic economic mix

Source PDF: all report pdf/France’s unrest adds to an already toxic economic mix.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Décembre 2018 53
Conjoncture française
Impact macroéconomique du mouvement des gilets jaunes :
une estimation difficile à ce stade
Estimer l’impact économique du mouvement des
gilets jaunes est un exercice difficile et incertain à la
date de finalisation de cetteNote de conjoncture (13
décembre 2018). Ce mouvement, qui a pris une
forme assez inédite, a en effet commencé à la
mi–novembre, soit relativement récemment, et se
poursuit encore au moment où s’achève la rédaction

de cette Note. Très peu d’indicateurs
macroéconomiques sont donc connus à ce stade.
Centré au départ sur la hausse des prix du carburant
automobile, le mouvement des gilets jaunes s’est
ensuite élargi à d’autres revendications, notamment
sur le pouvoir d’achat. Il a surtout pris la forme de
blocages routiers, mais aussi de manifestations
parfois émaillées de violence, à Paris comme en
province et outre–mer.
Si ce mouvement ne comporte pas de précédent

historique directement comparable, on peut tout de
même tenter de lister les canaux par lesquels il est
susceptible d’affecter l’activité économique.
Un impact probable sur la consommation,
notamment de services
Du côté de la production industrielle, les blocages de
routes et de dépôts pétroliers ont pu engendrer des
retards de livraison et cert aines entreprises peuvent
pâtir de problèmes d’approvisionnement.
Néanmoins, ces blocages semblent à ce stade d’une

ampleur moindre que ceux qui avaient affecté
l’activité économique pendant, par exemple, les
grèves massives de décembre 1995, lesquelles
avaient ôté environ 0,2 point à la croissance
trimestrielle du PIB.
En revanche, le mouvement des gilets jaunes est de
nature à pénaliser la consommation, localement
assez durement, et par plusieurs canaux. Tout
d’abord, les blocages, notamment routiers, ont pu
conduire les ménages à reporter ou annuler certaines

dépenses. Les dépenses en biens peuvent certes être
en partie reportées. Mais cet effet de report est en
général moindre pour les dépenses en services (en
particulier dans l’hébergement–restauration, les loisirs,
les transports) qui pourraient donc connaître des pertes
sèches plus marquées.
Un effet sur le tourisme
Car, aux effets directs des blocages, s’ajoute le
caractère anxiogène des s cènes de violence qui ont
marqué le mouvement, en particulier les samedis 24

novembre et, plus encore, 1er et 8 décembre. Certaines
se sont déroulées dans des lieux symboliques du pays et
hautement touristiques (avenue des Champs–Elysées et
Arc de Triomphe à Paris). On peut donc s’attendre à ce
qu’elles pénalisent les activités liées au tourisme.
La France a déjà connu des épisodes émeutiers, par
exemple en novembre 2005. Les données disponibles
suggèrent néanmoins que ces événements n’ont sans
doute pas eu d’impact significatif sur l’activité

touristique. Celles-ci concernaient alors la région
parisienne, mais pas le cœur touristique de la capitale.
À l’inverse, les attentats de novembre 2015 à Paris,
évènements de nature certes très différente, ont pesé
pendant plusieurs mois sur le tourisme, ôtant
0,2 point de PIB à la croissance annuelle de 2016 (cf.
Insee, 2016 et Beatriz, 2018).
À titre illustratif, la chronique des glissements annuels
des nuitées dans les hôtels figure sur le graphique

ci–dessous pour la période 2015–2016. La
fréquentation touristique dans l’hôtellerie a nettement
baissé après les attentats de novembre 2015
(graphique ). C’est – logiquement – la clientèle
étrangère qui a été la plus touchée. L ’attentat du 14
juillet 2016 à Nice a sans doute eu un effet du même
type, même s’il a été moins marqué. Au moins deux
autres évènements ont vraisemblablement eu aussi un
impact sur cette série durant la période considérée : les

manifestations du printemps 2016, dont la violence
ponctuelle n’était néanmoins pas comparable à celle du
1er décembre 2018, et, à l’inverse, l’Euro de football
organisé en France en juin et juillet 2016.

### Page 2

Conjoncture française
54 Note de conjoncture
En novembre 2018, la confiance des ménages
dans la situation économique a nettement
baissé
À côté des principaux indicateurs macroéconomiques,
les enquêtes de conjoncture auprès des entreprises et
des ménages permettent d’avoir une idée de la
manière dont les acteurs économiques envisagent leur
situation à venir.
Mi–décembre, les résultats des enquêtes de
conjoncture de l’Insee auprès des entreprises ne sont

pas encore disponibles pour la période postérieure aux
manifestations émaillées de violence. Le mouvement
des gilets jaunes ayant commencé le 17 novembre, les
chefs d’entreprise n’ont pas pu les prendre en compte
dans leur réponse à l’enquête de novembre.
L ’enquête de conjoncture auprès des ménages affiche
quant à elle une nette dégradation de l’indicateur
synthétique de confiance en novembre. Toutes les
composantes de la confiance sont en baisse marquée.

En particulier, le solde sur l’opportunité de faire des
achats importants chute en novembre ; or ce solde
peut être relié à la consommation future des ménages
(cf. éclairage : Que disent les ménages sur leur
perception des évolutions de leur niveau de vie dans
les enquêtes de conjoncture ?). Il laisse donc présager
une consommation relativement peu dynamique au
quatrième trimestre 2018, trimestre pourtant marqué
par des mesures fiscales (baisse de la taxe d’habitation

et des cotisations salariales) venant augmenter le
pouvoir d’achat des ménages.
Le mouvement social pourrait également
peser sur l’emploi
Enfin, le ralentissement de l’activité lié au mouvement
des gilets jaunes pourrait également affecter la
dynamique des créations d’emploi. Par exemple,
certaines embauches de contrats courts ou d’intérim
prévus dans les magasins à l’occasion des fêtes de fin
d’année pourraient finalement ne pas se concrétiser à

cause des blocages.
Au total, le scénario de cetteNote de conjoncture fait
l’hypothèse que le mouvement des gilets jaunes
pourrait ôter 0,1 point à la croissance du PIB au
quatrième trimestre 2018, via les secteurs d’activité
sans doute principalement touchés : commerce,
hébergement, restauration, transports… Cette
estimation est néanmoins soumise à beaucoup
d’aléas, ne serait–ce que sur la durée du mouvement.

Oct Edition: Choppy Markets, Intact AI

Overseas  
Technology

Jeff Pu, CFA 
SFC CE No. BNO719 
jeffpu@gfgroup.com.hk 
Henry Huang 
SFC CE No. BYA749 
henryhuang@gfgroup.com.hk

Market to remain choppy: Tech equities were volatile in the past month, with rotation 
toward hyperscaler and internet names. That said , s emi and memory (Korea) 
weakness reflected lower risk appetite due to rising bond yields, AI monetization/ARR 
visibility and L-T AI debates (i.e., Broadcom CEO warned that capital may now be a 
bigger constraint), even as company-specific catalysts continued to be strong. On the 
other hand, the positive was the acceleration of AI model scaling (i.e., Meta’s Muse 
Wonder, Gemini 4, etc). That said, w e view the pullback primarily as a reset in 
expectations rather than the evidence of AI deterioration. Despite light positioning, we 
expect the markets will remain choppy in the near term but would  accumulate low 
valuation & sustainable names  (i.e., HBM, non-TSMC foundries, CPUs, CPOs, etc) . 
In our preferred list, we added CLS, while removing MTK and Hon Hai.  
U.S. roadshow takeaways (week of Oct 5): Investor tone skewed neutral to slightly 
cautious, driven by conservative top ‑down macro views, U.S. election, and ongoing 
debates around the L-T AI demand. Positioning in AI infrastructure has come off peak 
levels. Even  so, many investors would accumulate on pullbacks, with interest 
concentrated in “Made in U.S.” names (INTC, LITE, COHR), U.S. memory suppliers, 
TSMC, and selective bottleneck themes — including ASE, ABF substrates, 
MRVL/SMTC, and Japanese MLCCs. 
OCP & earnings season in focus: The week is anchored by AI/semis — SEMICON 
West, OCP, and earnings from ASML, TSMC, etc. OCP reinforced the industry’s pivot 
toward rack‑scale infrastructure, with inference and agentic workloads driving demand 
across compute, memory, and networking. Scale‑up optics, NPO/CPO, and 800VDC 
power were key themes, while AMD’s CPU outlook and Helios/Helion underscored a 
secular CPU uptrend. Nvidia ’s use of PTFE for NVLink boards further supports our 
positive stance on PTFE (see PCB Spec in 2 H27, Oct 5).  On earnings, ASML 
delivered largely in -line results, GM and OP above consensus with revenue slightly 
miss, and raised full year guidance again and is exploring ways to increase capacity 
by >30% 2028 to fulfill strong customer demand, also extending the order visibility 
beyond 2028. For TSMC, we anticipate an inline quarter. 
September monthly sales/data: TSMC sales -0.6% MoM, slightly ahead. Rubin to 
accelerate and T3 ramp. PC forecast trimmed. Substrate/CCL upcycle with 
pricing/margin expansion. Aspeed revenue +101% YoY. Details on page 5. 
GFHK Tech Most Read:  1) Jeff Pu on ASIC Updates on TPU v9 ; 2) Michelle Jing 
on PCB Spec in 2H27; 3) Alicia Xia on CPO Acceleration; 4) Jeff Pu on NAND Re-
acceleration; 5) Jeff Pu on TSMC 3Q26 Preview.

GFHK Tech Important Stock Calls  
In the past one month, our key reports and stock calls are: 
Jeff Pu on ASIC Updates on TPU v9, reported date on Oct 13: We expect near-term MTK headwinds 
from TPU v9 delays but remain constructive on MTK/MRVL longer term. Humufish’s redesign could 
push tape-out to Jan -27, MP tape -out to 2Q27 and shipments to 1Q28, while EMIB -T yields could 
recover from 45–50%. Rising TPU allocations to Anthropic could constrain Gemini compute, though 
higher internal allocation in 2H26 may support future models. SemiAnalysis expects Anthropic/OpenAI 
to each add 5 –10GW in 2027 vs. a few GW for Google. For TPU v10 (4Q28 /1Q29), MTK/GUC’s 
Icefish remains the only confirmed mainstream SKU, while Broadcom-led Merope faces AMD/Marvell 
competition, with both potentially testing EMIB-T. 
Michelle Jing on PCB Spec in 2H27, reported date on Oct 5: Some market watchers expect Nvidia 
to shift from PTFE to hydrocarbon resin (HC) for the VR300 NVL576 switch tray. HC—one of the glass 
free alternatives within PTFE hybrid architectures—has only recently passed SI testing and has yet to 
enter PCB MP, leaving it roughly three months behind PTFE in qualification. On the contrary, we 
believe that PTFE offers superior performance for high frequency, high speed designs. With VR300 
NVL576 shipments expected in 2H27 and the final configuration likely set by end 26 —and with a 
leading Chinese PCB ma ker already achieving meaningful PTFE hybrid yields —we maintain that 
PTFE hybrid remains the most probable solution for next year’s switch board. 
Alicia Xia on CPO Acceleration , reported date on  Sept 29 : Nvidia’s CPO scale -out switch is 
progressing faster than expected, with FAU yields improving. We forecast CPO switch shipments at 
15k/16k/30k in 4Q26/1Q27/2Q27 and 100k in 2027. For Nvidia/AWS scale -up, CPO and NPO 
continue in parallel, with full NPO adoption expected at NVL576 launch. Feynman is also devel oping 
both variants, with CPO potentially using QM6 + six 6.4T OEs. We shift AWS Trainium 4 launch to 
1Q28 and forecast global scale-up OE demand at 6.5m/42m in 2027E/28E. 
Jeff Pu on NAND Re -acceleration, reported date on Sept 20 : According to TrendForce, NAND 
demand is stronger than expected as U.S. CSPs scale AI infrastructure, with eSSD demand nearly 
2x prior forecasts. We raise NAND contract price growth to +21%/+17%/+15% QoQ in 
3Q26/4Q26/1Q27, while hyperscaler inventories decline. Supply remains disci plined at 15%/21%/20% 
YoY bit growth in 2025 –27. Demand is supported by agentic AI, vector databases and KV -cache 
offloading, where eSSD offers lower cost/bit than DRAM and better power efficiency than HDD. 
Jeff Pu on TSMC 3Q26 Preview , reported date on  Oct 7 : TSMC shares hit record highs on 
expectations ahead of the print and potential Terafab collaboration. We expect largely inline 3Q26 
results, capex and full-year guidance, with softer 4Q26 margin (67.2%). Management should reaffirm 
2026 capex at the ceiling and mid-40s YoY growth, while we forecast 37% growth in 2027 on sustained 
AI/CPU/networking demand and price hikes. Key focuses include margins, long -term capex/U.S. 
expansion, large-reticle packaging and A14. We raise 2026E/27E EPS by 2%/8% on hig her N2/N3 
capacity and operating leverage, and roll to 20x 2027E P/E, lifting TP to NT$2,960.

Sector Report | Technology

September Monthly Data  
TSMC September sales of NT$511.9bn, -0.6% MoM and +54.6% YoY, with total 3Q revenue reaching 
NT$1.49T, +17.6 QoQ, slightly ahead of expectations of +14%. The solid performance was well 
supported by ASIC, A20 Pro, and Rubin. For 3Q26, we expect gross margin to be 67.8%, higher than 
guidance of 65-67% and consensus of 66.4% . For 4Q26, we expect another 12% QoQ growth with 
GM at 67.2%, both ahead of street’s estimates of +10% QoQ and 66% margin. Ahead of the earnings 
call, key focus include gross margin trajectory, long -term capex discipline, and the execution of its 
U.S. fab expansion. 
Rubin to accelerate:  Hon Hai revenue last month was NT$1.16T, +26 % MoM, +38% YoY, mainly 
driven by iPhone build.  Quanta and Wistron delivered -23% and -17% MoM respectively, due to AI 
racks’ shipping timing differences. Of note, Pegatron surprised the market with +143.2% MoM revenue 
surge fueled by initial GB300 rack shipments to neocloud customers including Lambda and Together 
AI. This reaffirms our thesis of robust NVL72 order momentum across Neoclouds. Looking ahead, 
VR200 is ramping now, with volume deployment accelerating into 2027. We maintain our forecast of 
75k and 92k NVL72 (GB+VR) rack shipments for 2026 and 2027, respectively. Concurrently, Wiwynn 
delivered another solid month with revenue expanding 20.0% MoM, underpinne d by resilient GP 
server demand alongside the ramp of Trainium 3. 
ASIC ramping up: On design service vendors fronts, driven  by T3 ramp, Alchip’s Sep revenue 
reached a record NT$9.9 8bn. Backed by secured incremental 3nm wafer allocation and improving 
yields, management raised its 2027 revenue guidance, now targeting growth above the previous 50% 
YoY baseline. That said, we push out T4 shipment timeline into 1Q28. GUC delivered a strong beat 
with monthly revenue reaching a record NT$7.145bn (+22.4% MoM, +97.8 % YoY), driven by robust 
Axion 2 CPU demand. Beyond its engagement with top 4 CSPs, multi -year order visibility into 2027 
and beyond is further bolstered by high-margin (likely TK1) automotive ASIC designs. 
PC forecast trimmed: NB shipments from top 5 ODMs were 9.7m in Sep, with 3Q26 shipments down 
17% QoQ and 26% YoY. Global PC OEM shipments contracted 20.1% YoY in 3Q26, worse than 
expected, post robust 1H26 pull -ins with high pricing points and weak macro. For 2027, we project 
the industry will decline a further 5% YoY. On the competitive front, tier -1 vendors such as Lenovo, 
Dell, and HP are not immune to this downturn, while entry-level laptops (low cost models from China) 
continue to see stronger relative demand.  
Substrate/CCL Upcycle Intact: Unimicron continued to deliver another all-time high sales with +5% 
MoM and 65% YoY. Supported by ABF upward pricing of ~15% in 2H26 (likely more next year) and 
higher mix of AI products, we expect a meaningful GM uptick for this quarter with momentum to sustain 
into 2027 . In the meantime, ZengDing’s revenue rose 28% MoM and 36% YoY mainly driven by  
iPhone, 1.6T mSAP, and Rubin’s HDI. On the CCL front, bolstered by ongoing price hikes and strong 
demand from GP servers, ITEC revenue grew 15.5% MoM and 111% YoY, and NanYa Plastics 
recorded +12.5% MoM and +55% YoY. Meanwhile, EMC and TUC both posted another single digit 
growth MoM on sales.

Downstream tech – Smartphone & Apple  
We maintain Global smartphone forecast of -14% in 2026  
According to IDC, the preliminary 2Q26 smartphone data points to a still ‑soft market with uneven 
regional dynamics, and their commentary continues to emphasize structural rather than cyclical 
headwinds. Smartphone shipments remain under pressure from weak replacement cycles, cautious 
consumer spending, and limited breakthrough features. IDC typically highlights that “growth is 
concentrated in the premium tier,” driven by ecosystem lock ‑in, while the mass market remains 
challenged by price sensitivity. They also tend to note that China and parts of emerging markets show 
only modest stabilization, not a full recovery, and that vendors are prioritizing margin discipline over 
volume.  
Overall, by factoring in higher iPhone 17/17 Pro volume, we fine tune our global smartphone shipment 
forecast to -14% YoY from -15% YoY. 
For 2027, we expect global smartphone shipments to be flattish YoY. That said, 2026 -2027 will be 
one of the most challenging periods for the industry, driven primarily by sharply higher memory prices, 
which have already pushed OEMs to reduce shipments and raise device prices.

iPhone 18 Pro/18 PM demand lukewarm  
iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max wait-time lukewarm: iPhone 18 Pro and iPhone 18 Pro Max pre-orders began 
on Sept 12 in the U.S. and many other countries. Generally speaking, the wait-time appears lukewarm, 
with: 1) 3–4 weeks for 256GB/512GB Pro Max across all colors, as well as Burgundy 1TB in the U.S.；
2) 2–3 weeks for Glacier 256GB/512GB Pro Max in China; 3) 1–2 weeks for Black/Glacier 1TB Pro 
Max in the U.S. ；4) immediate availability for Silver 1TB and mos t 2TB Pro Max models in the U.S, 
as well as most iPhone 18 Pro configurations except for Burgundy 256GB in the U.S. We attribute the 
lukewarm wait-time to limited spec upgrade, higher pricing (especially for large storage models), etc. 
We also expect the Pro Max to be impacted by iPhone Duo and the criticism of heavy weight.  
iPhone Duo feedback generally positive 
The iPhone Duo, priced at $1,999 in the U.S., opened pre‑orders on Oct 16 and officially launched on 
Oct 23. Early reviews were positive, highlig hting its polished folding experience, high ‑quality inner 
display, and improved multitasking in iOS 27, but also point to trade ‑offs including a heavier and 
thicker form factor, a visible crease, and a dual ‑camera. Due to hinge constraints, we are reducing  
the iPhone Duo EMS build plan from 7m to 6m units in 2026. We also lower the iPhone 18 Pro/Pro 
Max build plan to 72m units given the constraints associated with the adjustable‑aperture camera.

Sector Report | Technology

Downstream tech – PC 
Global PC units down to -12% in 2026, followed by another decline in 2027 
On Oct 8, IDC reported 3Q26 global PC shipments fell 20.1% YoY, missing street expectations of a 
10–15% decline. The sharper contraction was primarily driven by front -loaded demand in 1H26, 
elevated ASPs, and broader macro weakness. This aligns with our prior view that ODM build 
momentum moderated starting in end -2Q26, amid the rising concerns over channel inventory. 
Meanwhile, the top 5 ODMs also saw 3Q26 shipments decline 26% YoY. Looking ahead, we model 
4Q26 shipments to continue 20% YoY drop (flattish QoQ), underperforming normal seasonality. 
On the component front, we expect memory de -spec to normalize. End -users still require sufficient 
DRAM capacity (16GB+) to handle on -device AI workloads, and PC DDR 5 supply constraints have 
eased compared to Server’s. On pricing, new models will still have higher pricing points to pass 
through 2H component cost increases across memory, CPU, etc. That said, we anticipate price hikes 
will remain measured vs 1H26 given sluggish end-market demand. 
Overall, reflecting weaker than expected 3Q26 results post pull -ins and a muted 4Q outlook, we trim 
our 2026 PC shipment forecast to -12% YoY. For 2027, we project shipments to decline further by 5% 
YoY, as higher ASP and persistent macro headwinds continue to weigh on demand.

On the competitive landscape, tier -1 PC OEMs experienced notable market share erosion in 3Q26.

market share YoY. Apple was supported by its M5 lineup and the entry -level MacBook Neo, while 
ASUS benefited from aggressive commercial channel expansion. Notably, low -cost PC models 
manufactured in China continue to gain traction across China and Southeast Asia. Looking ahead, we 
expect value-tier models to remain the primary driver in sustaining ongoing PC shipments.

Upstream tech – AI Semi & Supply Chain  
U.S. CSP capex to be +85%/+53%/+20% in 2026/2027/2028 
Given capex expands further into 2027, CSPs free cash flow are undergoing significant pressure and 
turning negative except Microsoft, such that shifting to debt financing for their massive capex. In 2025, 
Top 5 CSPs’ total debt issuance reached $108bn, or 24% of total capex . Per our Addressing 2028 
Capex and AI Platform Value Shift report on Aug 6, we forecast Top 5 CSPs’ debt issuance to reach 
$257bn/$419bn in 2026/2027, or 31%/35% of total capex.  Assuming debt issuance to grow in 
2028/2029 at a mild pace, we believe U.S. CSPs' balance sheet (except Oracle) to remain very healthy, 
due to net leverage (EBITDA basis) of only 0.5 and the net debt-to-equity ratio of only 18% by 2029.  
Despite healthy balance sheet for most CSPs, we expect most to safeguard balance sheets beyond 
2027 amid widening spreads and FCF profiles, with the focus shifting to returns. Together with a swing 
factor of competition from Chinese LLMs, we expect U.S. CSPs to decelerate capex expansion. We 
forecast Top 5 CSPs capex of $1,512bn/1,739bn in 2028/2029, or +20%/15% YoY, down from +53% 
in 2027.

Server CPU TAM to grow at 49% CAGR by 2030 
As the orchestration core of AI infrastructure buildouts, the Server CPU has gained significant traction 
over the past few months. This is driven by the Agentic AI/inference trend, fueled by the breakthrough 
of OpenClaw and Anthropic’s ARR surging to $44bn  in April. For instance, in latest earnings calls, 
AMD projected a >35% CAGR for Server CPU TAM by 2030, and Intel said the shift toward agentic 
AI is driving a structural demand for CPU, where the GPU-to-CPU ratio is tightening from 8:1 in training 
to 4:1 in inference. Based on our estimates, assuming inference accounts for 90% of AI workloads 
and GPU-to-CPU ratio for AI servers to reach 1:1.2 in 2030, we now expect Server CPU TAM to grow 
96%/71% in 2026/2027, reaching $215bn by 2030 from $30bn in 2025, with a 5-yr CAGR of 49%.

CoWoS tracker: fine tuning upwardly for total capacity 
Following our earlier raise in May Edition on May 12 in which we revised up TSMC’s CoWoS capacity 
from 175KPM to 195KPM by end-2027, the Street has moved up to our numbers , and we have fine 
tune our estimate to 200KPM by end-2027 on our Sept Edition on Sept 14.  
For OSAT, we maintain our earlier view for Amkor’s capacity of 15KPM/25KPM/55KPM by end of 
2026/2027/2028, and ASE’s CoWoS capacity of 15KPM/45KPM/55KPM by end 2026/2027/2028.  
For fabless, we keep our CoWoS estimates largely unchanged for AI GPU/ASIC. For our accelerator 
model, we maintain +98% YoY 2027E, and for 2028E, we now expect it to reach the TAM of $1,000bn.  
In our ASIC Update report on Oct 13, we lowered our TPU units in 2028 to 10m, vs. 8.9m in 2027.

Upstream tech – Optical 
Strong demand for pluggable through 2028. 
We expect that the total demand for 800G/1.6T will reach 90mn/80mn in 2027, increased by 
80%/167%+, mainly driven by: 1) Nvidia, Google and AWS and other ASIC’s Accelerators’ demand 
are ramping up; 2) GPU/ASIC scale -out bandwidth continuously  increase drives increasing 
GPU/ASIC-to-optical module ratio; 3) We expect that TPU for 2027 will be 8.9m and Google fully 
transitions to large -scale clusters in 2026 and the scale -up layer will adopt optical interconnects. In 
addition, as we expect that CPO/NPO will start since 2H27, represents a pure incremental opportunity 
for optical interconnects supply chain, benefiting FAU, laser, shuffle box, connector and OE players, 
including LITE, GLW, TSEM, Sumitomo and Browave. We also like Marvell and SMTC, for the 
TIA/Driver used in NPO OE.  
Scale-out bandwidth increase driving optical-module deployment 
For NVIDIA, each Rubin GPU is equipped with two CX9 NIC chips, doubling the scale-out bandwidth 
compared with Blackwell. We further estimate that Rubin Ultra will adopt four CX9 chips per GPU, 
leading to another 2 times increase in scale-out bandwidth, continuing NVIDIA’s consistent bandwidth-
doubling trajectory. Accordingly, we expect the ratio of 1.6T optical modules has risen from 1:2.5 to 
1:5 in Rubin. From CoWoS perspective, we expect 5m/2m Blackwell/Rubin and 20m 1.6T in 2026. 
For Google, we believe that Goo gle fully transition to large -scale clusters in 2026 and the scale -up 
layer will adopt optical interconnects. We believe that in the scale-up domain, the ratio of TPU to 1.6T 
optical modules is approximately 1:1.5; In the scale-out domain, the ratio is around 1:2.5, suggesting 
the overall TPU-to-optics ratio is approximately 1:4 (1.6T equivalent).

Nvidia/CSPs’ Scale-out/up CPO/NPO  
Within NVIDIA’s next -gen NVL576 and broader CSPs’ AI infrastructure plans, CPO and NPO are 
developed side -by-side to expand GP U/ASIC interconnect bandwidth: CPO is the ideal long -term 
design for lower power and latency yet faces headwinds from complicated advanced packaging, and 
yield risks, while NPO could deliver most short -reach optical performance gains with far better 
manufacturability and deployment flexibility, making NPO a reliable near -term scaling path and CPO 
the ultimate end -state architecture. NPO partially removes module -side DSP and shifts signal 
conditioning and equalization to system designs, greatly lifting the value of TIAs and laser drivers that 
take over part of traditional DSP functions; we estimate each 3.2T optical engine contains tens of 
dollars worth of combined TIA and driver chips, a clear improvement over standard pluggable optics. 
Our shipment forecasts at 6.5m/42.4m NPO optical engines in CY27/28.  
Scale-up domain to increase by NVL576 to compensate lower memory: With lower memory spec, we 
expect Nvidia to increase the scale -up domain by optics to enhance performance. According to 
Nvidia’s GTC 2026, th e optical scale -up fabric allows Nvidia to expand Rubin Ultra to an NVL576 
scale-up. Regarding CPO and NPO which are being parallelly developed, we expect NPO to be a 
high reliability scaling path for NPO. We now expect its scale -up to adopt NPO in Rubin U ltra and 
Feynman platform and estimate OE shipments of Nvidia’s platform to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028.

### Page 25

Sector Report | Technology

Nvidia’s NPO OE to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028 
For Nvidia, we now expect its scale up to adopt NPO in Rubin Ultra and Feynman platform and 
estimate OE  shipments of Nvidia’s platform to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028, based on full NPO 
adoption at NVL576 launch given NPO maturity. Coupled with other CSP’s platform, we estimate total 
OE market to be 6.5m/42.4m in 2027/2028.  
TIA/Driver to benefit from NPO 
NPO partially removes module -side DSP and shifts signal conditioning and equalization to system 
designs, greatly lifting the value of TIAs and laser drivers that take over part of traditional DSP 
functions; we estimate each 3.2T optical engine contains tens o f dollars worth of combined TIA and 
driver chips, a clear improvement over standard pluggable optics.  
A key architectural shift under NPO is the partial removal of DSP functionality at the module level, with 
signal conditioning and equalization increasing ly redistributed into system -level design. This 
significantly elevates the importance of TIA (transimpedance amplifier) and laser driver components, 
which effectively replace a portion of traditional DSP value. We estimate that each 3.2T optical engine 
will embed high tens of dollars of combined TIA and driver content, representing a meaningful step -
up versus traditional pluggable optics architectures.  
From a competitive standpoint, we expect Marvell to remain the primary beneficiary of both NPO OE 
given i ts stronger positioning high speed TIA/Driver. Semtech is well positioned as a secondary 
beneficiary through its exposure to high-speed analog signal chain components, particularly TIAs and 
laser drivers, which scale directly with optical engine proliferat ion under NPO. We forecast 
6.5mn/42.4mn NPO OE shipments in CY27/28. Based on our estimated front-end content per OE and 
a 30% market share assumption for Semtech, we estimate its NPO revenue of $315/936mn. 
Stock Implication 
Generally speaking, scale-up CPO/NPO is an incremental opportunity for optical interconnect supply 
chain, as it aims to replace copper and has no impact on scale -out side. Additionally, scale up and 
scale out CPO solution share the similar suppliers. That said, we expect the key benefic iaries to be 
components such as FAU, CW laser, ELSFP, shuffle, PIC, TIA and Driver. We: 1) prefer Lumentum/ 
COHR due to the demand upside for CW laser, and also like local cw laser players; 2) expect Browave 
(3163 TT) to benefit, given the sizable contribu tion of CPO’s shuffle box considering ~55% market 
share, $5-6k ASP and a volume ramp in 2H26; 3) we also like TSEM, given CSP preferring NPO 
solution for their ASICs scale up interconnection. 4) expect GLW to benefit, given the second supplier 
of shuffle b ox and main supplier of MMC connector/fiber. 5) Marvell/Semtech will also benefit from 
CSP/Nvidia’s NPO solution, since TIA and driver will upgrade in the NPO Optical Engine.

### Page 28

Sector Report | Technology

Google’s 6D Torus & Beneficiaries.  
We expect Google will migrate its TPU interconnect from 3D to 6D torus, re -organizing the 64 -chip 
elemental block from 4× 4× 4 to 2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2. External optical links per block rise from 96 to 384, with 
optical ports per chip scaling increase from 1:1.5 to 1:6, driving 4x coherent lite transceivers and OCS 
demand. The trajectory aligns with NVIDIA's CPO/NPO move. Both are interconnect scaling that 
expands direct chip-to-chip bandwidth and reduces hop count, with TPU via a 6D torus and NVIDIA 
via CPO/NPO for Scale-up. 
Google’s migration from 3D  to 6D Torus: We expect Google to migrate its TPU interconnect from a 
3D to a 6D torus topology, re-organizing the elemental 64-chip building block from a 4× 4× 4 3D Torus 
cube into a 2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2 hyper-cube. In the current 3D design, each chip connects to 6 nearest 
neighbors across X/Y/Z axes and the cube exposes 16 optical ICI links on each of its 6 faces, totaling 
96 external optical links per cube (~1.5 optical ports per chip). Under the 6D design, each dimension 
has 64 external optical links (2⁶ chips, on e outward link per chip per dimension), for a total of 6 ×  64 
= 384 external optical links (~6 optical ports per chip). The per-chip optical port count thus scales from 
1.5 to 6. Combined with Scale -out at an estimated 1:2.5, this brings the aggregate TPU -to-port ratio 
to 1:8.5. 
Lower latency and closer to non -blocking Scale-up: For a 4,096 -chip superpod, a 3D torus (4× 4× 4) 
carries a worst-case diameter of 24 hops, whereas a 6D torus (2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2) halves that to 12 hops 
(2× 6), directly compressing collective-operation and latency. Higher dimensionality also spreads traffic 
across 6 orthogonal paths, improving bisection bandwidth and moving closer to non-blocking chip-to-
chip communication. Critically, shortening each edge from 4 to 2 nodes eliminates deep mu lti-hop 
electrical paths inside the block: the architecture deliberately reduces intra -cube copper 
communication and pushes a far larger share of traffic onto optical fiber. 
Key beneficiaries: The exact TPU generation for the adoption of 6D torus and initi al penetration rate 
remain unclear. That said, whether NVIDIA moves toward CPO/NPO or Google expands from 3D to 
6D, both are Scale -up strategies aimed at enabling greater inter -chip communication. Key 
beneficiaries include the 2.4T Coherent Lite module and the OCS supply chain, such as MEMS-based 
optical switch vendors, PIC/laser/DSP suppliers, and fiber/cabling vendors supporting the larger 
reconfigurable lightwave fabric.

Risks 
1) Alternative technologies; 2) AI demand and capex slowdown; 3) IT demand slowdown; 4) product 
delays.

Are investors rational?
The stock market strength and resilience over the last 3 months appears to be a rational response to strong
earnings and only limited signs that higher energy prices are materially affecting broad corporate profits.
Our dbLumina gauge indicates investors have been irrational during market

drops and periods of geopolitical tensions What does it mean to be ‘rational’?
Risk-off periods:
The tariff fears in 2025 and then the Iran
war in 2026 were key buying opportunities
in equity markets. Yet, the overwhelming
fear exhibited by investors in these periods
largely meant they sold off risk assets in an
emotional and irrational response.
Risk-on periods:
It can be rational to buy into a rising
market. Our indicator suggests this is what
happened in Q3 as earnings growth

continued to be strong.
Fear Indicators:
Sharp, disproportionate reactions to
threats, widespread sell-offs on
announcements, panic, flight to safety
beyond what might be fundamentally
warranted.

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 1
A reallocation of global sovereign reserve assets is accelerating, spurred by
geopolitical uncertainty and the fear of trade and financial sanctions. Notably,
central banks are repatriating their physical assets, such as the Dutch and French

central banks which have recently moved gold reserves out of New York.
Furthermore, a World Gold Council survey reveals that 45% of global central banks
plan to increase their gold reserves over the next 12 months.
What the market underestimates: The market views the recent surges in gold prices
as temporary, cyclical safe-haven flows. In reality, sovereign institutions are
structurally preparing for a more fragmented global trade system where tariff

policies and sanctions may make dollar-clearing assets more difficult.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Interest rates: At a time when the "world is awash in debt", long-term interest rates
of some countries may be forced structurally higher to attract alternative private
capital.
Inflation: Unexpected dollar depreciation may increase the cost of imported goods
Equity Prices: High-growth US equity valuations, which are highly sensitive to the

long-term risk-free rate, may face multiple compression if bond yields are pushed
higher.
The market currently prices in a material, long-term GDP boost from AI-driven
productivity gains. However, some research from the IMF suggests that over-reliance
on standardised LLMs can produce a high degree of "sameness" and uniformity in
cognitive output. Furthermore, escalating intellectual property and copyright
litigation threaten to bifurcate the quality of AI models based strictly on developers'

licensing budgets.
What the market underestimates: Some investors assume that the exponential
curve of AI hardware capabilities translates directly into exponential economic
output. But there is a risk that if AI tools merely standardise corporate processes
rather than innovate them, and legal barriers restrict access to premium data, the
productivity boom may be less aggressive than expected.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
GDP growth: Structural growth ceiling if AI fails to deliver the multi-percentage

point boost to global productivity required to offset aging demographics.
Equity prices: A downward revaluation of the technology and software sector if
massive AI infrastructure expenditures fail to generate revenue according to existing
expectations.
Geopolitical fragmentation of global reserve assets The AI “sameness“ trap and the productivity stagnation1 2
8

### Page 10

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 2
Advanced economies are facing a dual demographic crisis: rapidly aging populations
and underfunded pension systems. This is forcing some older people to remain in or
re-enter the workforce out of financial necessity rather than choice.

What the market underestimates: Lower unemployment rates are celebrated as a
sign of economic strength. However, in structurally ageing societies like Japan and
parts of Europe, the growth of the labour force is increasingly driven by part-time,
older workers. Furthermore, G20 nations lack adequate, cohesive policy frameworks
to address these long-term demographic fiscal drains.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: An older workforce working out of necessity has less discretionary

spending power, potentially locking economies into persistent, structural demand-
side deflationary forces, offset by supply-side wage pressures in highly skilled
sectors.
GDP growth: Labour productivity growth that is lower than it might otherwise be as
the median age of the workforce climbs, limiting long-term potential GDP.
The geopolitical battleground is focussed on energy blockades (such as the Strait of
Hormuz) but underestimates the weaponisation of critical minerals. China currently

holds ‘escalation dominance’ over rare-earth mineral supply chains. The emerging
risk is the proactive restriction of these minerals to Western firms, such as recent
bans on supplying rare-earth inputs to some US firms.
What the market underestimates: The market treats rare-earth supply constraints as
temporary trade spat negotiation tactics. In reality, as Western nations aggressively
push for reshoring and friend-shoring, China is increasingly using its processing

monopoly as a long-term strategic blockade to try to slow Western high-tech,
defence and the energy transition.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: Upwards pressure on inflation as the cost of manufacturing electric
vehicles, semiconductors, and advanced electronics surges due to supply blockades
and the high capital cost of rebuilding localised processing facilities.
Interest rates: Central banks may be forced to keep policy rates higher to combat

the persistent supply-side inflation generated by supply-chain fragmentation.
The fragmentation of the EU’s political centre Critical mineral isolationism and ‘green’ supply chain blockades3 4
9

### Page 11

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging themes – part 1
Our dbLumina AI model helps us identify the key emerging themes that have the potential to
most impact markets and economies in the short term
While the previous focus was on the AI hardware squeeze and
commercial competition, the narrative is shifting towards AI's role as a

critical component of national security and geopolitical leverage.
— AI access: We see this in President Trump's framing of AI development as a
geopolitical race ("whoever wins AI wins").
— AI as a bargaining chip: Several examples over the last 12 months including the
2025 deal whereby AMD agreed to pay 15% of sales from certain AI chips to the
US government in exchange for an export license. Even if these deals change, the
trajectory is one where access to AI technology is tied to national economic and

security interests.
— Direct military & security applications: Several examples including, cyberattacks
on critical infrastructure (such as a UK power plant), the use of AI in developing
"exceptional" phages to fight E.coli, and the growing field of AI security
discussions.
Outlook: Expect more government intervention, the use of AI technology access as a
tool in diplomatic negotiations, and a greater focus on the dual-use nature of AI in
both commercial and military contexts. This will likely create regulatory and

operational uncertainty for tech firms.
The trend of reshoring is evolving into a more proactive diplomatic
strategy where nations are forming new, often transactional, alliances
to navigate a world less dominated by a single superpower.
— Proactive alliance building: Canadian Prime Minister Carney has been front and
centre making his first international trip to France and the UK in an effort to
diversify away from traditional reliance on the US. That follows other key

meetings between Japan and India, and more.
— Bypassing traditional powers: Greenland signalled its willingness to turn to
China for investment if Western countries do not provide support, reinforcing the
leverage of some smaller nations.
— Shifting security arrangements: The US pushed for a ‘reassurance force’ in
Ukraine, while French President Macron hosted a "coalition of the willing" to
discuss Europe's defence without US guarantees. Further examples include the

discussion of a Franco-German "nuclear sharing" arrangement.
Outlook: The assumptions of a bipolar world order is giving way to a more complex,
multi-nodal system. This will create greater emphasis on regional blocs and issue-
specific coalitions. The stability of traditional alliances like NATO and the G7 remain
deeply uncertain.
AI’s impact on geopolitics and national security The fracturing of multilateralism and the rise of "Middle Power Countries" 1 2

### Page 12

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging themes – part 2
These four key themes dbLumina identifies are sometimes ‘known’ by investors, however, they may be underestimated by the market,
particularly when there are some potentially series longer-term impacts
Previous talk of “sovereign fiscal fragility” has now manifested itself

into a severe market reaction. Although fears around government debt
and deficits pop up from time to time, the magnitude of September’s
bond sell-off has been compounded by oil price concerns
— Accelerating sovereign sell-off: Concerns about inflation and debt sustainability
are not new but the peaks in bond yields are. Yields on 10-year Treasuries, bunds,
and gilts all rose sharply, with bunds hitting their highest level since 2009.
— Fiscal policy as a direct market catalyst: The market is increasingly sensitive to

fiscal policy announcements. For example, in the UK postponed tax rises, led to a
spike in gilt yields. Similarly, France's political turmoil and concerns over its
budget deficit caused the Franco-German yield spread to widen significantly.
— The “awash in debt" narrative: Treasury Secretary Bessent's comment at the G20
that "The world is awash in debt" signalled that the issue is increasingly top-of-
mind for policymakers.
Outlook: Markets will be increasingly quick to punish any perceived fiscal

irresponsibility. Expect higher borrowing costs, currency volatility, and increased risk
of financial instability, especially for countries with high debt levels.
The strain in the US consumer and labour markets is now diverging
sharply from the health of large corporations, reinforcing the K-shaped
economy.
— Strong corporate performance: Earnings season for Q2 showed remarkably solid
global growth, and the resilience of large corporations was a recurring theme

that helped support equity markets despite other headwinds.
— Weak consumer & labour signals: Economic reports have showed some signs of
a weakening labour market, falling consumer confidence, and stress among low-
income Americans. The New York Fed's survey also showed households expect a
worse financial situation.
— Policy and market disconnect: While strong corporate performance depends on
a robust economy, the underlying consumer weakness is concerning. Expect the

market to continue its twitchy reaction to data, as investors struggle to reconcile
these conflicting signals.
Outlook: This theme is potentially the most consequential for the domestic US
economy. If corporate profits and equity markets "detach" from the real economy as
consumers perceive it, it could lead to significant social and political instability,
making economic policy even more difficult to navigate.

Source PDF: all report pdf/Quantum dictionary, part 1_ Decoding the fundamentals From superposition to decoherence, we unpack the concepts and technologies shaping the future of quantum computing. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America

### Page 1

13030420     1

Transformation 
Quantum dictionary, part 1: Decoding the fundamentals 
21 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• What is quantum computing? How does it work? What is a qubit? And when will quantum reach its full potential? Quantum 
computing challenges our intuition and our understanding of what computers can do. In this two-part dictionary series, we 
decode key terms behind what could be the next major technological revolution. 
• Qubits are the basic building blocks of quantum computers, harnessing properties such as superposition and entanglement to 
process information. However, qubits are highly sensitive to their surroundings where disturbances can lead to errors in 
quantum computation, making error correction a key challenge for building quantum computers. 
• There is no single approach to building a quantum computer. Researchers and companies are pursuing a range of qubit 
technologies, from neutral atoms and photons to spin, superconducting, topological and trapped-ion qubits, while quantum 
computing itself is likely to increasingly work alongside classical computing and AI.

First, let’s lay the groundwork… 
Quantum computing  
Quantum computing uses qubits and quantum properties such as superposition (defined later on) to perform complex 
calculations and solve certain problems faster than classical computers.1 From a practical perspective, quantum computing could 
be used to tackle problems related to optimization, machine learning, simulation and factorization across a range of sectors, 
including autos and logistics, financial services, energy and climate, health and life sciences and national security. 
Current quantum computers can perform ‘just’ thousands of quantum operations. As the technology advances from thousands to 
millions, billions and eventually trillions of operations, quantum computers could begin to have commercial applications.  
• Millions: Financial services and security could experience some of the first use cases, such as threat detection, fraud 
prevention, derivatives pricing, bond trading and arbitrage. 
• Billions: Quantum could help power AI through advanced data generation capabilities.  
• Trillions: Quantum chemistry applications could emerge, including drug discovery, battery simulation and materials 
discovery. 
These are the publicly known applications, according to Oxford Quantum Circuits (OQC). And as quantum computing capabilities 
improve, some of these use cases could emerge sooner than anticipated.  
Now, we’ll tackle the core concepts… 
Qubits 
Qubits (quantum bits) are a quantum computer’s basic unit of information. They can exhibit quantum properties such as 
superposition and entanglement (which we elaborate on below) — properties fundamental to quantum computing. 
Quantum gates manipulate the state of qubits, much like logic gates manipulate bits in classical computers. Despite the name, a 
quantum gate is not a physical object, but rather an operation applied to a quantum system that changes its state. 
From physical to logical qubits 
Physical qubits are the foundation of a quantum computer’s hardware. However, they are highly susceptible to error. To address 
this, multiple physical qubits can be structured together to encode a logical qubit, allowing errors to be detected and corrected

1 Schneider, J., & Smalley, I. (2025, June 10). What is quantum computing? IBM. 
I NSTI TUTE
Accessible version

### Page 2

2 21 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

and making the stored quantum information more reliable. In recent years, according to BofA Global Research, logical qubits 
have become a greater focus for quantum computing companies. 
Entanglement 
Entanglement is a condition where two or more qubits become linked so that their states are correlated, regardless of distance 
(Exhibit 1). It allows quantum computers to process information in ways that can be difficult or impossible for classical 
computers. 
So, how does it work? Suppose we have two photons that have opposite polarizations (the orientation of their light waves). 
Before measurement, neither has a definite polarization. When one is measured as horizontally polarized, the other will be 
vertically polarized when measured in the same basis, wherever it is. When measured separately, they appear to be random, but 
when compared, they are correlated (Exhibit 2). 
The challenges 
Once created, entanglement must be protected from decoherence (where interactions with the environment destroy quantum 
correlations). Maintaining entanglement over long distances is another challenge. Photons traveling through optical fiber can be 
absorbed or scattered, making long-distance quantum communication difficult. 2 Quantum repeaters aim to overcome this 
challenge by extending entanglement across a series of shorter links. 
Exhibit 1: Entanglement is a state where at least two qubits become 
correlated 
An infographic of entangled qubits

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 2: Measuring one qubit in an entangled pair instantly 
determines the state of the other, no matter the distance 
An example of what entanglement means for qubit measurement

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Superposition 
Unlike a classical bit, which can be one of two states (0 or 1), superposition allows a quantum system to exist in multiple states 
or configurations at the same time (Exhibit 3). For example, a qubit can exist in the 0 state, the 1 state or any state that is a 
combination of 0 and 1.3  
A quantum system can maintain a superposition until it is observed or measured. Once measured, its superposition collapses into 
a single state. Another way of thinking about superposition is to imagine a sphere with the north pole representing zero and the 
south pole representing one. Other points on the surface of the sphere represent different superposition states. This allows us 
to: 1) represent complex forms of information; and 2) simulate atoms in a computer. 
Schrödinger’s cat 
In 1935, physicist Erwin Schrödinger proposed a thought experiment to illustrate the strange implications of quantum 
mechanics. In this scenario, a cat, a radioactive substance that decays very slowly, and a Geiger counter are put together in a 
sealed box. According to quantum mechanics, the system can be described as a superposition in which the cat is both alive and 
dead until the box is opened. Once the box is opened and the system is observed, the superposition collapses into a single 
outcome.

2 Rehman, M. (2026, May 11). What is Quantum Entanglement? Quantum Insider. 
3 Microsoft. (n.d.). Superposition.

### Page 3

21 October 2026    3

I NSTI TUTE

Enabling parallel computation versus classical computing 
Superposition is a key attribute of quantum computing that allows for quantum parallelism, or the ability to perform multiple 
computations at the same time. Classical bits can only be in one of two states, 0 or 1. In contrast, a qubit can be in a 
superposition of 0 and 1, allowing a quantum computer to process multiple possible qubit states at once.4  
Exhibit 3: Superposition is the condition where a qubit can exist in a superposition of 0 and 1 
Illustrating the power of superposition

Source: BofA Global Research 
Note: A Bloch sphere is a geometric representation of qubit states. 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Error rates and fidelity 
Fidelity is a measure of how closely a quantum state or operation matches what was intended (Exhibit 4). A higher fidelity 
generally means fewer errors. 
Physical qubits are prone to errors. One way to address this is to combine multiple physical qubits to create a more reliable 
logical qubit. Quantum error correction (QEC) allows errors in the physical qubits to be detected and corrected, helping protect 
the information stored in the logical qubit.

4 Ibid.

### Page 4

4 21 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 4: Fidelity measures how accurately a qubit retains its intended quantum state during computation 
Infographic showing the implications of high versus low fidelity

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
How does quantum error correction work? 
At its simplest, QEC relies on redundancy, or storing information across multiple qubits so that errors can be detected and 
corrected.  
Consider an example: suppose someone wants to send a message represented by 0. Instead of sending a single 0, the message 
could be repeated three times as 000, known as a codeword. If one bit were accidentally flipped from 0 to 1 during transmission, 
the recipient might receive 010. Because two of the three bits are still 0, the recipient could infer that the intended message 
was 000. 
However, if two bits flipped, producing 110 or 011, the recipient could infer the wrong message. If all three flipped to 111, the 
recipient would have no way to know from the received codeword that errors had occurred.5 This approach is known as a 
repetition code and illustrates how redundancy can help protect information from errors.  
Repetition codes are a simple form of error correction that can address bit-flip errors, where a 0 changes to a 1 or vice versa. 
More sophisticated QEC codes are needed to address other quantum errors, such as phase flips.6 Shor’s code, developed by 
American theoretical computer scientist Peter Shor, uses nine physical qubits to encode one logical qubit and can correct one 
bit-flip error or one phase flip error, but it can’t correct both types of errors at the same time. Meanwhile, Steane’s seven-qubit 
code, developed by Oxford physicist Andrew Steane, can also correct both types of errors. It has the advantage of being fault-
tolerant, meaning the error-correction process itself does not introduce additional errors.7 
At what error level might we start seeing quantum advantage? 
Some classical computing systems today operate with extremely high reliability, often described as having "17 nines" of fidelity 
(99.99999999999999999%). By comparison, quantum computers currently achieve fidelity rates of around 99.9%. Gerald 
Mullally, CEO of OQC, has argued that quantum systems would need to add roughly three more nines of fidelity to achieve 
commercial relevance, or quantum advantage. 
Noise and decoherence 
Noise refers to unwanted disturbances that affect quantum systems and can lead to errors in computations (Exhibit 5). It can 
come from sources such as interactions with the environment, electromagnetic interference and temperature fluctuations. 
Coherence is a quantum system’s ability to maintain its quantum properties, including superposition and entanglement. When 
coherence is lost through interactions with the environment, the process is known as “decoherence,” causing the system to 
behave more classically (Exhibit 6).8

5 Microsoft Ignite. (n.d.) Introduction to quantum error correction. 
6 Ibid. 
7 Ibid. 
8 Quandela. (n.d.) Quantum Decoherence.

21 October 2026    5

I NSTI TUTE

To fully control qubits for calculations and information transfer, they need to be carefully protected from their surroundings. 
External factors like noise, energy, changes in Earth’s magnetic field, radiation from Wi-Fi or mobile phones or cosmic rays can 
interfere with the process. Even small disturbances can lead to decoherence, making it difficult to develop large-scale, fault-
tolerant quantum computers.9 
According to QuEra Computing, quantum noise can be mitigated through physical isolation of qubits, the development of more 
precise control techniques and quantum error correction, which helps detect and correct errors in physical qubits.  
Exhibit 5: Noise refers to unwanted disturbances, e.g., interactions 
with the environment, that lead to errors in quantum computation

An infographic of decoherence

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Quantum processing unit (QPU) 
Referred to as the brain of the quantum computer, the QPU is the core hardware where qubits reside and where quantum 
computations take place. QPUs rely on quantum behaviors like superposition and entanglement to tackle problems in areas such 
as cryptography, simulation and optimization. Just as a CPU (central processing unit) in classical computing contains multiple 
components, a QPU includes quantum gates (operations that manipulate qubits in a controlled way, similar to logic gates in 
classical computing) and interconnects, which enable communication between qubits and other quantum processors.10 
Next, we’ll look at broader computing architecture… 
Heterogeneous computing  
Heterogeneous computing refers to a future computing landscape likely combining classical and quantum computing, potentially 
unlocking more powerful, faster and cheaper compute and helping solve problems that classical computers cannot. According to 
BofA Global Research, AI and quantum computing could also converge and complement each other, enabling more 
transformative use cases. 
Quantum modality  
A quantum modality refers to the type of physical technology used to create and manipulate qubits. We discuss the modalities in 
greater depth in the following section. 
Lastly, we outline qubit technologies… 
Neutral atom qubit 
A quantum modality that uses individual atoms with no electrical charge as qubits (Exhibit 7). The atoms are held in place using 
focused lasers known as optical tweezers. Neutral atom qubit systems do not require atoms to be ionized, which means fewer 
energy constraints and potentially simpler cooling requirements. Atoms such as rubidium or cesium are typically used. The qubits 
are manipulated by changing the energy levels or positions of the trapped atoms using laser pulses.

9 QuEra. (n.d.) Quantum Noise. 
10 SpinQ. (2025, January 16). What does a Quantum Computer Look Like? Explain Key Parts.

  I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 7: A quantum modality where atoms with a net electrical charge of zero are the foundation for qubits 
Key features of neutral atoms

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Photonic qubit 
A quantum modality that uses particles of light, known as photons, to carry quantum information (Exhibit 8).11 Optical 
components such as beam splitters, which divide light along different paths, and phase shifters, which change the phase of light, 
can be used to perform quantum operations. Photonic quantum computers can use integrated photonic chips to manipulate 
photons and enable quantum logic. 
Exhibit 8: A quantum modality relying on the quantum properties of light 
Key features of photonic qubits

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

11 Moore, J. (2025, March 28). The 6 different types of quantum computing technology. TechTarget.

### Page 7

21 October 2026    7

I NSTI TUTE

Spin qubit 
A quantum modality that uses the intrinsic spin of electrons or nuclei in solid-state materials, such as silicon or diamond. These 
spins can be manipulated using magnetic or electric fields, and their states are read using optical or charge-sensing techniques. 
Spin qubits are particularly appealing because they can leverage existing CMOS (complementary metal-oxide semiconductor; a 
technology to build processors) manufacturing infrastructure, making it easier to integrate them with conventional processors. 
Superconducting qubit 
A quantum modality in which electronic circuits create qubits. They are built using Josephson junctions, where two 
superconductors are separated by a thin insulating layer. Microwave pulses manipulate the qubits’ quantum states to run 
computations.12  
Specifically, a resistance-free current oscillates back and forth around a quantum loop. An injected microwave signal excites the 
current into superposition states.13 To enable this process, superconducting materials are cooled to near absolute zero (around 
10-20 millikelvin) using dilution refrigerators, helping reduce thermal noise and maintain coherence. 
Topological qubit 
A quantum modality that aims to encode quantum information using topological properties rather than relying only on individual 
particle states (Exhibit 9). One way to think about this is through the paths certain quasiparticles take as they move around one 
another, creating “braids” in space-time. Because the information can depend on the overall pattern of these braids rather than 
small details of their paths, the approach is designed to be more resilient to certain disturbances. 
Exhibit 9: Resilient to small perturbations but requires special quasiparticles not yet been conclusively demonstrated in nature 
An infographic of topological qubits

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Trapped-ion qubit  
A quantum modality in which ions (electrically charged atoms) are held in place using electromagnetic fields. These ions act as 
the qubits and are controlled by the system with tightly focused laser beams.14 The qubits are held inside ultra-high vacuum 
(UHV) chambers.

摘要：
此前市场极度拥挤的 “ 做多 AI ，做空其他⼀切 ” 交易正在经历痛苦的解构，创纪录抛售下，超⼤规

10 ⽉ 22 ⽇，⾼盛欧洲固定收益与股票（ FICC & Equities ）交易主管 Rich Privorotsky 在位于伦敦
的⾼盛交易室接受了播客栏⽬《 The Markets 》的访谈。 Privorotsky 透露了⼀个极具冲击⼒的数
据：“ 上周我们的主经纪商业务账簿（ Prime book ）上，出现了有史以来最⼤规模的科技股抛
售。 ”
⾯对这波抛售潮，正在进⾏中的三季度财报季显得尤为关键。市场的焦点已从单纯的 “ 讲故事 ” 转

向了严苛的业绩兑现。 Privorotsky 指出，市场预计明年 AI 的资本⽀出（ Capex ）将⾼达 1.1 万亿
美元，但这已不⾜以让投资者买单。
“ 仅 仅 说 你 花 了 很 多 钱 是 不 够 的 ， 你 还 需 要 证 明 未 完 成 的 订 单 正 在 转 化 为 实 质 性 的 收
⼊。 ”Privorotsky 强调， “ 他们必须在推动资本⽀出的同时展⽰这些⽀出的回报。这不是⼀个轻松
的局⾯。 ” 他表⽰，云业务的增⻓、变现能⼒以及⾃由现⾦流实现收⽀平衡的速度，将对⽬前极其
敏感的信⽤市场产⽣重⼤影响。
基础设施与资⾦双重掣肘：明年存在 “40-50 吉⽡ ” 电⼒缺⼝
在 AI 狂热降温的背后，是现实世界中基础设施和资⾦池的硬性约束。市场⽬前正在与三个核⼼问
题作⽃争，⾸当其冲的便是资⾦成本的溢出效应和电⼒供应。

宏观范式重塑：从 “ 储蓄过剩 ” ⾛向 “ ⽀出过剩 ”
站在宏观周期的⻆度， Privorotsky 描绘了⼀幅深刻的范式转换图景。
他表⽰，⾃⼰的整个职业⽣涯都处于 “ 储蓄过剩 ” 的环境中 —— 系统⾥的钱多到不知道该怎么办。
但现在，情况发⽣了 “ 地震般的剧变（ Seismic shift ） ” 。
“ 在过去的⼀年⾥，我们预计将在 AI 资本⽀出上花费超过 1 万亿美元，⽽全球⼤多数政府都在运⾏
财政⾚字。我们正处于⼀个 ‘ ⽀出过剩 ’ 的时代。 ”
这种转变推⾼了资本成本，并⾃然⽽然地挤出了处于边缘、资质较差的借款⼈。
他观察到，这种压⼒不仅体现在 CCC 级信⽤利差的⼤幅⾛阔上，甚⾄蔓延到了主权债务领域

（如⾯临⾼额财政⾚字的法国国债）。再加上居⾼不下的能源价格，整体市场的⻛险偏好正在被
显著削弱。
市场情绪⾛向两极， “ 成也 AI ，败也 AI”
在复杂的宏观和微观交织下，投资者的焦虑情绪出现了奇特的 “ 极化 ” 现象。
Privorotsky 分享了他本周与投资者交流的⻅闻： “ 我遇到⼀些投资者说， ‘ 我担⼼⽆法交付算⼒，
这让我害怕 ’ ；然后我⼜遇到了另⼀批投资者，他们说， ‘ 我担⼼我们会过度交付算⼒，这让我害
怕 ’ 。不知何故，两个完全互斥的结果构成了市场担忧的总和。 ”
由于能源价格对经济产⽣重⼤影响（标普 500 虽然表现平稳，但中位数股票实际上已下跌超过
10% ），⾦融状况指数（ FCI ）⼤幅收紧，迫使美联储采取更多⾏动。
Privorotsky 曾坚信 “ 原油决定⼀切（ As goes oil, so goes everything else ） ” ，但他现在承认，在
当前的市场语境下，逻辑已经演变为 “AI 决定⼀切（ As goes AI, so goes everything else ） ” 。

尽管短期内⾯临压⼒，但 Privorotsky 并未完全悲观。他认为现货市场的算⼒价格每天都在创出
新⾼，需求依然 “ 贪婪且永⽆⽌境 ” 。在交易策略上，他表⽰虽然⽬前信念度不⾼，但已遭遇⼤幅
降级的 “ 电⽓化 ” 相关资产、⺠⽤航空航天资产以及部分运输板块，⽬前已经具备了可观的安全边
际，值得保持谨慎乐观。
以下为播客对话全⽂翻译：
Chris Hussey 00:00
这⾥是《The Markets》。我是  Chris Hussey，今天是  10 ⽉的星期四。我从伦敦⾼盛交易⼤厅请到
了  Rich Probarowski，他是  Prevo 旗下  FIC 与股票业务的欧洲股票交易主管。⾮常感谢你来做
客。谢谢你们的邀请。Hussey，我们就直接进⼊正题吧，因为，你知道的，你写的东⻄我全都读
了，⽽且你提出过⼀个很好的观点 —— 今年夏初的时候，那个痛苦交易，也就是⼤家不得不去追
的那个交易，是做多  AI、做空其他⼀切。它已经有点变成了、很⼤的⼀笔交易。现在随着我们
进⼊三季报季，这个交易似乎正在⽡解。到底发⽣了什么？你认为接下来会怎么演变？股票市场

出现了极其严重的集中度，⽽且我得说，过去⼏周对这类⼀致性交易来说尤其痛苦。

Rich Privorotsky 00:48

受美伊互相攻擊油輪 、胡塞組織控制曼德海峽及沙
烏地阿拉伯東西向輸油管遇襲關閉等待修復 ，預估

元，回到3月美伊衝突後的高峰 。由於4Q26油價高
機率將維持在90美元以上，預估將對航空公司運量
與獲利將造成負面衝擊。
雖然機構將工業產業 4Q26、1Q27獲利年增率從
7/1 的 4.3% 、 18.4% 微 幅 上 調 到9/18 的 7.6% 、

20.1%。上半年在美伊衝突下帶動國防類股大漲 ，
帶動工業產業預估本益比達到近30年的高點27倍。
雖然近期工業受到高油價與國防政策利空已經回檔
7%，但9/22預估本益比仍高達 22.6倍，高於過去
五年平均20.9倍，也高於整體標普500的19.1倍。

軍事行動 ，對國防類股形成
壓力
《油價與運價維持高檔，航空產業需求遭遇逆風》
近期受到中東衝突升溫導致僅有少量油輪通過荷姆
茲海峽，貨櫃船實際上仍無法通行。過去5-6年全球
港口吞吐運量成長遠低於船舶訂單量成長 ，導致重
大港口的貨櫃塞港嚴重 。中東地緣政治風險和全球
性的港口擁擠預估短期內都未能改善契機 ，因此未
來半年貨櫃運價仍將維持高位 。由於過去半年美國
機票價格已大幅上漲 ，導致價格敏感的美國旅客消
費開始偏向保守。美國運輸安全管理局(簡稱TSA)統
計每日旅客吞吐量近期呈現持續下滑 ，顯示航空業
持續性漲價已經衝擊到旅客需求 。隨著機構上調

4Q26原油預測 10%到88美元，高價位的航空燃油
價格也將再次對全球航空公司運量與獲利造成衝擊 。
《眾議院通過限制擴大對伊朗軍事行動 ，對國防類
股形成壓力》
今年上半年因中東衝突升溫及美伊緊張情勢 ，國防
股曾出現一波強勁上漲 。但隨著美國眾議院 9/15以
220票對204票通過限制總統對伊朗軍事行動的決議
案，要求未獲國會授權前不得持續或擴大對伊朗的
軍事行動 。雖然美國國會決議案主要是限制新增的
軍事行動授權 ，並非削減國防預算 。但美國國會限
制進一步軍事行動 ，已經造成市場資金對國防類股
進行獲利了結 。考量航空類股仍將面臨高油價逆風 ，
以及美國眾議院擴大限制政府對伊朗軍事行動 。本
行對工業產業展望維持中立評等。
資料來源：TSA、JP Morgan，2026/9/17，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/24

### Page 1

算力新瓶頸，光互連新局：
CPO 進入量產驗證期
產業聚焦
2026/09/17
• 短期商用確定：1.6T 模組放量、
交換器 CPO 落地
• 中期瓶頸突破：異質封裝良率
與可維護性成焦點
• 長期潛力可期：光學 I/O 仍需
等待 Tape-out 認證
AI叢集擴張，網路效率決定GPU利用率
大型模型訓練由數千顆GPU擴張至數萬甚至更大規模後，系統瓶頸已由單顆晶片效能延伸至Scale-up、
Scale-out與跨資料中心互連，交換器頻寬、傳輸延遲、每位元功耗及故障率若未同步改善，GPU會因等
待資料而閒置，新增資本支出也難以轉化為有效Token產出。市場衡量標準因此由GPU數量，進一步轉向
每瓦、每埠及每個Rack可穩定傳輸的資料量，光通訊價值也隨節點數、網路層數與連線密度同步上升。
雲端客戶的採購重點將更偏向有效算力、網路uptime與總體擁有成本，而非只比較名目GPU規模。
可插拔與 CPO 並行，2026 年迎商用量產拐點
TrendForce估計AI光模組市場由2025年165億美元增至2026年260億美元，800G以上出貨占比逾六成，

短期收入仍由800G／1.6T模組、EML／CW雷射、DSP與FAU領先。另一方面，NVIDIA Spectrum-X
Ethernet Photonics已進入量產，Broadcom Tomahawk 6大量出貨並以Davisson推進102.4T CPO，聯
電亦完成SILITH 1.6T矽光子晶圓首批交付。產業已跨過技術展示階段，但CPO仍會先集中於高Radix交換
器。可插拔、NPO與CPO將依距離、功耗、維修及供應鏈控制需求並行，收入不會在單一年度完成替代，
2026至2027年的主旋律將是分層導入成熟Scale-out技術。
量產與毛利率驗證優先，光I/O保留長線彈性
SEMICON Taiwan 2026把焦點由PIC效能推進至晶圓製程、異質封裝、主動對位、多層測試與現場維修
，顯示競爭核心轉為能否以可控成本大量製造。選股宜先檢查平台與客戶、量產時點、收入占比、良率、
毛利率及資本支出回收，再評估2030年TAM。2028年前後若Optical I/O進入XPU封裝，ASIC設計、光

子Chiplet、先進封裝及Rack整合可望取得更高價值量，但仍須等待Tape-out、標準與系統驗證。台灣供
應鏈的核心觀察值應由合作公告轉為晶圓出貨、封裝一次通過率、Rack部署與毛利率變化。
CPO在AI資料中心的滲透率持續提升

### Page 2

AI萬卡叢集瓶頸：網路效率決定有效算力
Scale-up與Scale-out承擔不同任務
Scale-up連接同一運算域內的GPU／XPU，主要交換梯度、參數與記憶體資料，追求極低延遲
、高雙向頻寬與高Radix，現階段由NVLink及短距離銅互連主導，UALink生態持續推進。
Scale-out則跨Rack或跨叢集擴張節點，重視可擴充性、可靠度與距離，主要採Ethernet或
InfiniBand，已直接帶動800G與1.6T光模組。Scale-across再把不同資料中心連成訓練與推論
Fabric，對長距離光連結、同步與容錯提出更高要求。其中集合通訊對尾端延遲特別敏感，任一
鏈路壅塞或重傳都會同步拖慢運算域；Scale-out則更在意東西向流量、超額訂閱比與故障切換。
光學價值量隨節點、層數與連線密度增加
當Tier-1 Switch由Rack內移至End-of-Row，且單通道由100G升至200G，PCB走線、連接器與
銅纜損耗快速提高，系統必須加入DSP、Retimer及低損耗材料。電光轉換位置因此由面板模組

移向Switch ASIC，形成LPO、NPO與CPO，下一階段再以Optical I/O延伸多Rack Scale-up。
價值鏈不再侷限於光模組，並擴大至ASIC、矽光子PIC、雷射、FAU、先進封裝、測試、系統組
裝與液冷。採購決策也從單一元件規格轉向端到端鏈路預算、每瓦頻寬、可維修性與總持有成本；
能同時提供電、光、封裝與系統驗證資料的供應商將具備更高競爭門檻。
【注意事項】本行所提供之資訊僅供參考用途。本行當盡力提供正確之資訊，所載資料均來自或本諸我們相信可靠之來
源，但對其完整性、即時性和正確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資

AI互連由晶片內部延伸至跨資料中心
資料來源：NVIDIA、Broadcom、Ayar Labs；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

網路層級 範圍 核心要求 光學演進
Scale-up 同一運算域 最低延遲、高雙向頻寬 短銅線、NPO、Optical I/O
Scale-out Rack至叢集 高Radix、可靠度、可擴充 800G／1.6T、Switch CPO
Scale-across 資料中心之間 長距離、同步、容錯 相干光、DWDM
記憶體／I/O 封裝至Rack 低pJ/bit、頻寬密度 光子Chiplet、UCIe光連結

### Page 3

埠速與架構分流：可插拔守量，CPO攻高頻寬
埠速升級：800G維持主流，1.6T進入量產導入期
800G以8×100G/lane為主，供應鏈與客戶驗證相對成熟，1.6T進入8×200G/lane，隨IEEE
P802.3dj與OIF規範推進，已成為2026年AI資料中心升級重點，3.2T預計朝8×400G/lane發展
，仍需突破SerDes、DSP、光源、散熱與面板密度限制，埠速代表單一介面的傳輸能力，並不
直接等同CPO滲透率，短期營收仍會先反映在可插拔模組與關鍵光電元件。不同CSP亦會依交換
器週期、銅纜距離與功耗預算錯開升級，因此同一年度仍將並存多種埠速；供應鏈應以design-
in、認證完成與量產坡度判斷實質貢獻。
架構遷移：光靠近晶片後，價值轉向封裝與系統
LPO移除模組內DSP以降低功耗與延遲，保留可插拔維修性，但通道容忍度較低，NPO把光引擎
置於ASIC鄰近區域，在電氣距離與模組化之間折衷，CPO將光引擎與Switch ASIC整合於同一封
裝，優先解決高Radix交換器的功耗與面板密度，Optical I/O再把光子Chiplet導入XPU封裝，用

於多Rack Scale-up與記憶體解耦。四種方案的量產時點、供應鏈與毛利結構不同，評價不宜混
在同一TAM。投資上最先可驗證的是量產BOM、出貨顆數與良率，封裝內光I/O則需另看Tape-
out、標準與客戶共同設計。

需求增量來自埠速與連線密
度同步提升
AI Cluster擴張不只提高單埠速度，也
增加GPU節點、Leaf／Spine交換器層
數與每顆加速器對應的光連線數，因而
光學需求呈現量與價同步上升，最先受
惠環節包括800G／1.6T模組、EML／
CW雷射、DSP、Driver／TIA與FAU光
纖陣列。需求能見度仍受GPU交付、交
換器建置及網路拓樸架構影響。
CPO開始進入特定商用量產，不會立即
排擠可插拔模組，反而先擴大整體光學
市場價值量，兩者將於不同架構層級長
期共存。供應鏈評估仍應清楚區分既有
量產收入、客戶認證中的新品，以及尚
未形成標準採購規格的長期方案。追蹤
時宜同步比對總埠數、每顆GPU光連結
數、單通道ASP與生產良率之變化。

CPO商用起步：2026量產、2028–2029加速
CPO由低基期進入商用
曲線，2028年後加速
TrendForce估計AI資料中心CPO滲
透率由2026年約0.55%升至2030年
35.74%，增量主要集中於2028至
2029年Scale-up導入，CPO／
NPO市場規模則可由2025年約1億
美元增至2030年逾390億美元。由
於早期滲透率高度受少數美系雲端
龍頭與運算平台牽動，單一大型客
戶的驗證時程或拉貨節奏調整，即
可實質改變全年的成長斜率。
低基期與平台量產同時出現，代表
2026年是商用起點而非獲利高峰，
近期應先觀察Switch出貨、每台光
引擎數量、良率與客戶部署，而非
把2030年TAM提前一次反映。只有
Field Return 與維修機制通過驗證
後，規模化滲透才具可持續性。

5
架構分工由距離、功耗與維
修成本共同決定
可插拔模組具標準成熟、可快速熱插拔
更換與多供應商生態優勢，1.6T乃至未
來3.2T仍會在主流Scale-out網路層維
持龐大出貨規模；NPO有效縮短電氣距
離，同時保留部分模組化彈性；CPO優
先用於高Radix、超高功耗及面板密度
受限的旗艦級Switch；Optical I/O則鎖
定跨Rack Scale-up運算域與XPU封裝
內部互連。
四條路線更可能長期分工，而非單一路
線全面取代。滲透率斜率將取決於功耗、
系統良率、維修標準與總持有成本。採
購決策還受故障域（Blast Radius）影
響：可插拔可局部更換，CPO若維修恐
需停用整張交換主板，節能紅利極易被

備品儲備與停機損失抵銷，使現場可維
護性成為放量前提。

### Page 6

200G通道縮短銅線距離，CPO降低功耗
電氣損耗降至約4 dB
傳統可插拔架構讓 200Gb/s 訊號由
Switch ASIC 穿越基板封裝、PCB
走線、面板連接器與 Cage 模組後
才進入光收發器。NVIDIA 指出，此
完整電氣路徑的損耗最高可達 22
dB，系統必須在板上加入大量 DSP、
Retimer 與超低損耗板材以維護訊
號完整性，進而推高板層數與散熱
成本。當通道進一步朝 400G/lane
推進時，高頻插損與串擾的設計裕
度已逼近極限。CPO將光引擎移至
Switch ASIC鄰近封裝，將高頻電氣
損耗大幅降至約 4 dB，因而成為
51.2T 至 102.4T 以上高 Radix 交換
器的重要演進方向。未來銅線將退
回封裝基板內或極短距板內傳輸，
跨板與跨機櫃鏈路將逐步提高光連
結占比，封裝內與極短距板內仍將
由電氣互連主導。
200G電氣路徑損耗由22 dB降至約4 dB
資料來源：NVIDIA ；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

6
光路分光結合外置雷射，介
面功耗降至 9W
NVIDIA 資料顯示，傳統 1.6T 可插拔光
模組介面功耗約達 30W（DSP 20W +
EML 10W），而 CPO 架構可將介面功
耗驟降至約 9W（光引擎 7W + 外置雷
射源 2W），此一大幅節能效益，除來
自省去高速 SerDes 驅動長走線的電氣
功耗外，關鍵更在於光路架構的革新：
CPO 採用外置連續波（CW）雷射，透
過矽光子晶片內部分光機制，將每
1.6Tb 頻寬所需的雷射數量由傳統可插
拔的 8 顆 EML 大幅縮減至 2 顆外置光
源，在降低功耗的同時亦將熱源移出交
換器核心。對百萬瓦級 AI Factory 而言，
網路用電下降能直接釋放更多電力與冷

卻空間供 GPU 運算，但系統端也必須
納入外置雷射 ELSFP 壽命、可拆式光纖
連接與現場維修（Field Return）機制，
以確保整體 TCO 真正獲得優化。
光互連功耗由約30W降至約9W
資料來源：NVIDIA；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

400G逼近極限：純矽、TFLN與InP共存
3.2T／204.8T轉向400G
通道
下一代3.2T光模組與204.8T交換器
將朝400G/lane推進，元件須同時
提高調變頻寬、降低VπL與插入損
耗，並維持雷射效率、熱穩定與晶
圓均勻度。隨PAM4接近頻寬極限，
驅動器線性度、接收靈敏度與FEC延
遲也將影響鏈路預算。
Broadcom已展示400G/lane
Taurus DSP搭配400G EML／PD，
先支援低功耗1.6T並建立3.2T基礎；
聯電與SILITH則規劃把純矽MZM延
伸至400G/lane。速度提升不只考
驗單顆元件，也考驗PIC製程、封裝、
光源與測試共同收斂，量產觀察值
應包括頻寬、功耗、良率與客戶驗
證週期。若實驗室性能無法在溫度
循環與晶圓分布下維持，則量產良
率與交期將面臨挑戰。
光靠近晶片後，頻寬密度提高、能耗下降

7
純矽、TFLN與InP依效能及
成熟度分工
純矽MZM具CMOS相容、12吋晶圓與
成熟供應鏈優勢，適合規模化與成本控
制，但高速效率與驅動電壓仍需優化；
TFLN具高頻寬、低損耗與較佳調變效
率，聯電、SILITH及HyperLight正推進
量產平台，封裝與材料均勻度是關鍵；
InP可整合高效率雷射、光放大器
（SOA）與高速調變器，適合主動光元
件，成本、熱與良率門檻較高。
材料平台絕非單一勝者全拿的零和競爭，
最終取決於系統規格、封裝整合成本與
能否導入一線客戶長約供應鏈。實務上
將朝異質整合（Heterogeneous
Integration）演進：由矽基承擔被動
波導與大規模封裝整合，TFLN負責高

速調變，InP則專注提供高效雷射光源
與訊號放大，發揮各自最佳物理特性。
400G/lane材料平台比較
資料來源：Broadcom、UMC、Soitec；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

SEMICON Taiwan 2026：矽光子轉入量產競賽
矽光子論壇議程由元件效能延伸至完整光子Fabric
SEMICON Taiwan 2026矽光子國際論壇以Die-to-Die至Rack-to-Rack為主題。Cisco、Marvell
與Lightmatter先討論Scale-up、Scale-out及Scale-across架構，說明網路已成為影響XPU利用
率與每瓦Token產出的系統資源；台積電以COUPE說明EIC與PIC透過SoIC整合，聯電把
200G/lane延伸至400G/lane與TFLN，日月光、imec及Soitec則聚焦光電異質整合。議程把設
計、製程與系統放在同一條量產鏈上，反映CPO已進入工程整合階段。
真正稀缺能力轉為可重複製造流程
Lam、Onto、ficonTEC、Advantest與Enli把討論推進至薄膜、折射率、側壁形貌、晶圓篩選、
雙面電光測試、主動對位與翹曲補償。產業問題已由「光能否取代部分銅線」，轉為「晶圓、封
裝、光纖耦合與系統能否在可控成本下大量生產」。2026年後的競爭指標不只Tb/s，也包括

Known Good Die、整體良率、測試時間、外置雷射可靠度與現場維修性。能把多個環節串成穩
定流程的供應商，更可能先取得收入與毛利率。設備商雖可在終端CPO出貨前取得工程線訂單，
但收入可能隨驗收而波動，真正可持續的供應商須累積製程資料庫、縮短對位與測試秒數。

異質封裝拉長製程：KGD與良率相乘成關鍵
EIC、PIC、ASIC與光纖
共同封裝放大失效率
傳統光模組可以獨立測試與更換，
CPO則把Switch ASIC、EIC、PIC、
雷射介面、透鏡、FAU與大尺寸基
板納入同一系統，任何一顆光引擎
或一條纖維通道失效，都可能造成
高價值組件報廢，整體良率也會受
到多個子系統良率相乘影響。當單
一封裝含多顆光引擎時，即使各子
系統良率看似良好，整體良率亦會
因乘數效應而大幅滑落。
ASE的典型流程涵蓋PIC／EIC後製、
晶圓或裸晶鍵合、光學組裝、基板
整合及最終系統測試，橫跨電性、
光學、熱與機械公差，量產管理難
度高於單一元件，製造端還須建立
逐站追溯，把晶圓座標、鍵合批次
與最終通道表現連結。
CPO流程橫跨晶圓、CoW、光學與系統組裝

先測再組與Known Good
Die決定成本結構
量產策略必須在高價值ASIC封裝前剔除
壞Die，並在光引擎組裝前後重複確認
電性與光損耗。晶圓級篩選可降低後段
報廢，主動對位與原位校準決定光纖耦
合效率，翹曲與熱應力控制影響最終一
次通過率；外置雷射與可拆式纖維則關
係到現場維修時間。若測試秒數過長，
良率提升也可能被設備折舊與在製品占
用抵銷；若coverage不足，則會導致
高昂的客退與返工成本。
供應鏈評價應從單顆元件規格，延伸至
良率爬升速度、UPH、返工率、測試成
本與Field Return。只有這些關鍵指標
同步改善，CPO才具備擴張至更多埠數
的經濟性。能以數據閉環把缺陷回饋至
晶圓與組裝製程的廠商，才能在良率爬
升曲線中脫穎而出。

### Page 10

設備商搶先受惠：雙面電光量測與對位前置
晶圓至系統須建立多層電光測試
矽光子雖常採45／65nm等成熟線寬，光學效能卻高度依賴薄膜厚度、折射率、波導側壁、應力
與材料均勻度，製程控制並不簡單。Onto與Enli等方案把薄膜、形貌與光損耗映射前移至晶圓或
裸晶階段，先識別異常區域；ficonTEC強調重組晶圓、裸晶與模組需導入雙面電光量測、原位校
準、翹曲補償及自動對位；Advantest則推進可擴充、標準化的NPO／CPO測試架構。設備投資
因此早於最終出貨放量，量測資料若能與晶圓座標、封裝批次及系統序號連結，便可建立由缺陷
來源至Field Return的追溯閉環，以統計製程控制縮小規格分布。
商業化關鍵指標由標稱頻寬轉向UPH與Field Return
測試覆蓋率不足會把壞Die帶入昂貴封裝，覆蓋過度又會拉長測試秒數並降低UPH，量產必須在
成本與風險間取平衡，應追蹤晶圓與光引擎良率、主動對位精度、整機一次通過率、每顆測試時
間、ELSFP壽命、維修工時與Field Return，相關機會將擴散至薄膜量測、光譜影像、探針與治

具、主動對位、自動化組裝及Burn-in，若標準遲未收斂或客戶採垂直整合，設備需求也可能呈
現專案化與收入波動，設備訂單通常由實驗室、工程線、複製線再走向量產線，與終端營收可能
相隔數季，評價時應區分單機開發、批量複製、耗材治具及維護服務。

光學I/O入XPU封裝：2028年前先看NRE
光子Chiplet延伸Scale-
up距離
光學I/O把電光轉換由交換器旁移入
GPU、XPU或ASIC封裝，透過UCIe
等晶粒介面連接光子Chiplet，可望
在較低pJ/bit下提供數十至逾
100Tb/s的封裝級頻寬，並支援記
憶體解耦與跨機櫃Scale-up，其經
濟性來自以少量光纖取代大量高速
電氣I/O，釋放封裝邊緣與主板走線
資源。
Intel已展示整合式OCI光子晶粒，
Ayar Labs則以TeraPHY推進CW-
WDM光學介面與UCIe連結。相較
Switch CPO，光學I/O更接近運算
晶片與封裝共同設計，單機價值量
與技術門檻都更高。不過光源供應、
封裝熱耦合、記憶體一致性與軟體
拓樸仍須同步驗證。
Intel OCI光子Chiplet整合至運算封裝

開放標準決定CPO能否由單一平台擴大採購
Open CPO建立Rack系
統藍圖
Lightmatter與19家公司於2026年8
月在OCP啟動CPO系統架構倡議，
發布約300頁基礎白皮書，目標建立
符合MHS與Open Rack v3的共同藍
圖，使Scale-up域由72顆擴張至
1,024顆以上節點，並支援十萬顆
XPU叢集。共同藍圖可降低每家
CSP自行定義接口的重工。
隨SerDes通道速率逼近448G，銅
線極限傳輸距離驟減至數十公分以
內，促使標準化範圍由單顆光引擎
封裝，進一步擴大至交換器、XPU
運算板、外置光源、光纖理線、高
功率散熱、供電及模組化維修介面。
對台廠供應鏈而言，跨廠商互通性
測試（Interop）與現場可維護性將
成為新一代量產認證的核心門檻。
台灣系統與光元件業者已加入Rack級驗證

14
規格收斂決定第二波滲透率
OIF以3.2T CPO及200G/lane介面推進
光引擎、外置雷射、連接器與散熱；
OCI MSA希望讓不同XPU、Switch與
光學供應商互通；IEEE 802.3dj則建立
200G/lane Ethernet實體層基礎。多套
規格並行有助擴大供應來源，也可能造
成接口分歧與認證重工。對台廠而言，
標準化雖可擴大可服務市場，也會提高
價格透明度與第二來源壓力。
2026至2027年間，關鍵觀察指標聚焦
於各標準規格凍結時程、跨廠商互通性
測試ELSFP外置光源模組壽命及可拆式
光纖連接器之量產驗證。待產業標準全
面收斂後，CPO採購模式方能由封閉的
垂直整合旗艦平台，順利擴展至多供應

商的公有雲市場採購體系。能穩定交付
驗證樣品並累積大量互通數據實績之業
者，方能在規格定案放量後率先取得首
波規模化採購訂單。
四套開放規格的分工與進度

台灣供應鏈依營收時點分層，量產證據最關鍵
2026至2027年先看已有平台與出貨實績的環節
台積電以COUPE、SoIC與CoWoS切入光電整合，聯電與SILITH已完成12吋1.6T PIC首批量產交
付，日月光投控／SPIL具備CPO封裝、組裝與測試布局，鴻海與FIT則由光纖互連延伸至Switch
及Rack系統。波若威等被動光元件廠的機會來自FAU、Fiber Shuffle與可拆式連接，但仍須確認
客戶認證及CPO收入占比。這些公司具較明確平台位置，評價重點應落在出貨量、良率、營收占
比與毛利率，晶圓代工與OSAT雖較早受惠前置開發，獲利仍要看量產批次是否持續，系統與光
纖廠較接近終端部署，認列則受機房建置與驗收影響。
2027至2028年再看ASIC、Rack與Optical I/O驗證
創意與Lightmatter等合作推進CPO ASIC，世芯-KY布局跨Rack Scale-up，緯穎展示Optical
I/O Compute Tray，設備與測試商則受惠晶圓篩選、雙面電光量測、主動對位及Burn-in需求。

此層的單案價值量與獲利彈性較高，收入時點也更依賴Tape-out、封裝驗證、客戶認證與標準
收斂。投資排序須區分量產、系統驗證與合作公告。設備商可能由工程線先行受惠，但若規格反
覆或客戶垂直整合，訂單也可能停留在少量專案，須再追蹤同機型複製台數、耗材維護及第二廠
區導入。

### Page 16

產業方向確立，投資回歸出貨、良率與毛利率
量產鏈優先，OpticalI/O保留長線選擇權
部分Switch CPO已進入量產部署，產業整體仍處量產爬坡、良率改善與系統驗證並行，Optical
I/O則仍以NRE、Tape-out及平台驗證為主，2026年最確定的收入仍是800G／1.6T可插拔模組
與關鍵雷射，NVIDIA與Broadcom量產則確認Switch CPO形成第二條成長曲線。台灣晶圓代工、
封裝測試、光纖互連與系統整合具直接位置，設備商可在良率爬升前取得需求。Optical I/O進入
XPU封裝後價值量更高，較可能於2028年前後具規模，應等待Tape-out、封裝與客戶認證。投
資順序可依可見度分成三層：已量產光模組與光源、進入平台部署的Switch CPO，以及仍待
Tape-out的Optical I/O，前兩層重視出貨進度與實質獲利貢獻。
催化劑與風險須同步追蹤
近期催化劑包括Vera Rubin／Spectrum-X出貨、1.6T放量、Tomahawk 6與Davisson部署、

SILITH晶圓交付、COUPE／CoWoS共同設計、OCP首批規格及400G/lane元件驗證。主要風險
則為1.6T／3.2T可插拔延壽、CPO整體良率與外置雷射可靠度低於預期、標準分歧、客戶提高自
製比例，以及估值領先收入。整體產業展望維持正向，選股仍應依序檢查客戶與平台、量產時點、
收入占比、良率、毛利率及資本支出回收。若高速可插拔持續降功耗、CPO維修成本偏高，導入
斜率可能低於預期，反之，規格收斂、第二客戶複製與Field Return下降，將推動導入斜率加速
超越預期。

歐洲市場問答：高利環境下的壓力與機會
1
Thematic Report
重點提示
中東地緣衝突自2026年2月底爆發以來仍陷僵局，能源價格維
持高檔的時間較預期更長，令歐元區通膨壓力與升息預期升溫。
另一方面，歐洲政府擴大財政支出，公債供給增加，加上歐洲
央行持續縮表，推升期限溢價，令歐洲公債殖利率大幅走升，
德國十年期公債殖利率9/28升破3.6%，創2009年6月以來新高。
本報告透過問答，分析歐洲公債殖利率走高的原因、主權債務
與財政風險、政府因應措施、高利率對企業的影響，以及高利
率環境下的歐洲股債展望。
◼ 本波歐洲公債殖利率走高的驅動因素：能源價格上漲推升通
膨、市場重估歐央貨幣政策路徑，同時，財政支出令歐洲公

債供給增加與歐央持續縮表使私人部門需要吸收更多公債供
給，推升期限溢價，財政疑慮使部分歐洲國家利差承受額外
壓力，加上全球利率上升的外溢影響，共同推升殖利率走高。
◼ 歐洲政府調整發債結構，央行備有穩市工具：歐洲政府縮短
發債期限以控制融資成本，預估此趨勢將延續至2027年；
萬一主權債市場出現無序波動，歐洲央行有OMT與TPI政策
工具，但不會輕易動用。
◼ 整體歐洲債務風險尚可控但中長期壓力持續累積：目前利率
水準尚未對整體債務可持續性構成普遍威脅，但財政脆弱性
較高的國家壓力升高，法國尤其需要關注。財政與政治變數
使法國資產風險溢價維持高位，目前股債定價已反映相當程
度的負面風險。
◼ 德國公債殖利率短期將維持高檔：歐洲央行12月仍有再升息
可能，待能源價格與通膨壓力緩和、升息預期降溫後，利率
才有回落空間，但難降至3%以下。
◼ 歐洲企業資產負債表健康、獲利具韌性，緩衝高利率衝擊。
◼ 高利率限制歐股估值擴張，歐股維持中立，聚焦獲利成長與
政策支撐的產業：銀行受惠於目前利率環境與信貸需求，工

業、電氣化/能源安全及AI受惠戰略自主與資本支出提升。
Sep. 29, 2026
林亞瑱

能源價格大漲引發政策路徑重估為利率上揚主因
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/9/15，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/9。
德國公債殖利率曲線上移，短率升幅大於長率
中東衝突發生以來德國公債殖利率曲線(中東地緣衝突前 vs. 目前)
近期全球公債殖利率大幅上揚 ，歐洲國家亦然，德國10年期公
債殖利率9/28升破3.6%，創2009年6月來新高，若從2月底中

東地緣衝突爆發以來起算 ，德國十年債利率累積彈升了近 100
個基點，30年期德債利率亦逼近4%(至9/28止)。
Q1：什麼因素推動今年歐洲公債殖利率走高？
(1)能源價格上漲推升通膨、市場重估歐央貨幣政策路徑，為驅
動本波利率上揚的主因：
2月底中東衝突爆發以來 ，德國10年期公債殖利率與油氣價格
走勢高度相關。此外，德國公債各期限殖利率全面上移 ，其中
短率升幅高於長率 ，反映本波利率上揚主 要受到能源價格上升
所帶動的通膨風險，以及市場對ECB政策路徑重新定價的影響。
此外，美國殖利率上升透過全球資本市場產生外溢效果 ，帶動
全球無風險利率中樞上移 ，亦對歐洲公債殖利率形成上行壓力 。

歐洲政府發債規模增與ECB縮表同步推升債券供給
■淨債券發行 ■ECB縮表 ◆對私人部門的淨供給
(2)歐洲公債供給增加、央行持續縮表，推升期限溢價：
德國擴大財政支出，歐洲政府融資需求及公債供給增加；同時 ，
ECB持續進行資產負債表正常化 ，逐步減少公債持有量 ，使新
增債券供給需由私人投資人吸收 ，推升投資人要求的期限溢價 。
除此之外，政府債務持有者結構轉變 ，個人與海外投資人等對
價格較為敏感的投資人占比增加，使債市更容易受到市場情緒
與金融條件變化的影響。
(3)財政疑慮使部分歐洲國家利差承受額外壓力：
歐洲部分國家面臨較高的政府債務壓力，加上政治局勢限制財
政調整空間，市場對債務可持續性的疑慮難消 。尤其在年度預
算協商期間，財政脆弱性較高的國家如法國 、英國，主權債務

風險重新定價容易反映在長端殖利率及與德債的利差擴大。
